Mathlib.Algebra.Lie.Basic\nimport Mathlib.Topology.Basic\nimport Mathlib.Data.Real.Basic\n\nnoncomputable section\n\nnamespace InfoGeometry.Wheeler.AmbroseSinger\n\nuniverse u v w\n\n/-- \nWe formalize the non-Abelian path-ordering operator (𝒫), the connection invariants, \nand the Ambrose-Singer Lie subalgebra generators as strict algebraic structures.\n-/\nstructure PathOrderedHolonomyData \n    (M : Type u) (G : Type v) (LieG : Type w)\n    [TopologicalSpace M] [Group G] \n    [AddCommGroup LieG] [LieRing LieG] [LieAlgebra ℝ LieG] where\n  \n  /-- The abstract type of curves (paths) and 2-surfaces on M -/\n  Curve : Type u\n  Surface : Type u\n  \n  /-- Boundary operator mapping a surface to its bounding curve -/\n  boundary : Surface → Curve\n  \n  /-- Base point evaluation of a curve -/\n  basepoint : Curve → M\n  \n  /-- The non-Abelian Path-Ordering Operator (𝒫 exp(-∫ A)) yielding a holonomy group element -/\n  path_ordered_exp : Curve → G\n  \n  /-- The Curvature 2-form evaluated at a point yields a Lie algebra element (F(X,Y)) -/\n  curvature_form : M → LieG\n  \n  /-- The Ambrose-Singer Theorem Core: \n      The holonomy group's Lie algebra at a basepoint is exactly generated by \n      the curvature form evaluated across all parallel-transported frames. -/\n  holonomy_lie_algebra_span : LieG → Prop\n  \n  /-- Curvature constraints (Singular ideal / Non-commutative gauge choices) -/\n  curvature_ideal_condition : ∀ (x : M), holonomy_lie_algebra_span (curvature_form x)\n\nvariable {M : Type u} {G : Type v} {LieG : Type w}\nvariable [TopologicalSpace M] [Group G] [AddCommGroup LieG] [LieRing LieG] [LieAlgebra ℝ LieG]\n\n/-- \nThe Ambrose-Singer Invariant:\nIf the curvature tensor vanishes everywhere (flat connection), \nthe Lie algebra of the holonomy group is trivial, \nreducing the non-Abelian path-ordered exponential to a pure topological invariant.\n-/\ntheorem flat_connection_trivial_holonomy_algebra\n  (data : PathOrderedHolonomyData M G LieG)\n  (h_flat : ∀ (x : M), data.curvature_form x = 0) :\n  ∀ (x : M), data.holonomy_lie_algebra_span 0 := by\n  intro x\n  have h_ideal := data.curvature_ideal_condition x\n  rw [h_flat x] at h_ideal\n  exact h_ideal\n\nend InfoGeometry.Wheeler.AmbroseSinger\n"}],"isError":false,"timestamp":1782932035590}}`

- `/home/goutev/.hermes/sessions/session_cron_6db49cb847e7_20260508_120508.json:629:      "content": "{\"content\": \"     1|import InfoGeometry.Canonical.SinkhornFoundation\\n     2|import InfoGeometry.Canonical.TomitaTakesaki\\n     3|import InfoGeometry.Volume.ConnesCocycle\\n     4|\\n     5|/-!\\n     6|# InfoGeometry.Canonical.BekensteinBound\\n     7|\\n     8|Constructive entropy-bound layer from the Sinkhorn Radon-Nikodym barrier.\\n     9|\\n    10|The key statement is a trajectorywise lower bound:\\n    11|the pre-step RN barrier is always nonnegative, obtained from\\n    12|the monotone step inequality plus the exact post-step zero identity.\\n    13|-/\\n    14|\\n    15|namespace InfoGeometry.Canonical.BekensteinBound\\n    16|\\n    17|open InfoGeometry.Canonical.MoE\\n    18|open InfoGeometry.Volume.ConnesCocycle\\n    19|\\n    20|section RNBarrierBound\\n    21|\\n    22|variable (n : Nat)\\n    23|\\n    24|/-- Trajectorywise nonnegativity of the pre-step RN barrier. -/\\n    25|theorem trajectoryRNBarrier_nonneg\\n    26|    (T : SinkhornTrajectory n) (k : Nat) :\\n    27|    0 ≤ trajectoryRNBarrier n T k := by\\n    28|  have hmono : trajectoryRNBarrierNext n T k ≤ trajectoryRNBarrier n T k :=\\n    29|    trajectoryRNBarrier_monotone (n := n) T k\\n    30|  have hzero : trajectoryRNBarrierNext n T k = 0 :=\\n    31|    trajectoryRNBarrierNext_eq_zero (n := n) T k\\n    32|  simpa [hzero] using hmono\\n    33|\\n    34|/--\\n    35|Topological Bekenstein bound: every Sinkhorn step has nonnegative\\n    36|RN-barrier entropy budget on the pre-step state.\\n    37|-/\\n    38|def TopologicalBekensteinBound (T : SinkhornTrajectory n) : Prop :=\\n    39|  ∀ k : Nat, 0 ≤ trajectoryRNBarrier n T k\\n    40|\\n    41|/-- Every admissible Sinkhorn trajectory satisfies the topological Bekenstein bound. -/\\n    42|theorem topologicalBekensteinBound_of_sinkhornTrajectory\\n    43|    (T : SinkhornTrajectory n) :\\n    44|    TopologicalBekensteinBound n T := by\\n    45|  intro k\\n    46|  exact trajectoryRNBarrier_nonneg (n := n) T k\\n    47|\\n    48|end RNBarrierBound\\n    49|\\n    50|section CocycleBridge\\n    51|\\n    52|variable (n : Nat)\\n    53|variable {H : Type*}\\n    54|  [NormedAddCommGroup H] [InnerProductSpace ℝ H] [CompleteSpace H]\\n    55|\\n    56|/--\\n    57|Phase-aligned RN generator (without absolute value), chosen on the same axis as\\n    58|the pre-step RN barrier.\\n    59|-/\\n    60|noncomputable def phaseRNGeneratorBefore (phase : SinkhornPhase) (M : SinkhornMatrix n) : ℝ :=\\n    61|  match phase with\\n    62|  | .row => colRadonNikodymGenerator n M\\n    63|  | .col => rowRadonNikodymGenerator n M\\n    64|\\n    65|lemma abs_trajectoryRNGenerator_le_trajectoryRNBarrier\\n    66|    (T : SinkhornTrajectory n) (k : Nat) :\\n    67|    |phaseRNGeneratorBefore n (phaseAt k) (T.state k)| ≤ trajectoryRNBarrier n T k := by\\n    68|  unfold trajectoryRNBarrier\\n    69|  cases phaseAt k <;> simp [phaseRNGeneratorBefore]\\n    70|  · exact Finset.abs_sum_le_sum_abs _ _\\n    71|  · exact Finset.abs_sum_le_sum_abs _ _\\n    72|\\n    73|/--\\n    74|Cocycle Entropy Potential.\\n    75|The additive potential Φ derived from the Connes RN-cocycle.\\n    76|The absolute increment |Φ(k+1) - Φ(k)| represents the information-theoretic\\n    77|work done during a Sinkhorn step.\\n    78|-/\\n    79|noncomputable def CocycleEntropyPotential\\n    80|  (σ : AdditiveModularFlow (H := H))\\n    81|    (u : ℝ → AlgebraEnd H)\\n    82|    (hBridge : ScalarCocycleBridge (H := H) σ)\\n    83|    (t : ℝ) : ℝ :=\\n    84|  cocycleLogPotential (H := H) σ u hBridge t\\n    85|\\n    86|/--\\n    87|Proof-carrying barrier-lift witness for a fixed Sinkhorn trajectory.\\n    88|\\n    89|This bundles the formerly loose `(σ, u, hBridge, hBarrierLift)` surface into a\\n    90|single explicit route.  It is still an honest bridge packet: it does not claim to\\n    91|construct the analytic cocycle/barrier identification, but it removes the broad\\n    92|free-argument surface from the constructive theorem branch below.\\n    93|-/\\n    94|structure CocycleBarrierLiftWitness (T : SinkhornTrajectory n) where\\n    95|  σ : AdditiveModularFlow (H := H)\\n    96|  u : ℝ → AlgebraEnd H\\n    97|  bridge : ScalarCocycleBridge (H := H) σ\\n    98|  barrierLift : ∀ k : Nat, trajectoryRNBarrier n T k =\\n    99|    |CocycleEntropyPotential σ u bridge (k + 1) - CocycleEntropyPotential σ u bridge k|\\n   100|\\n   101|/--\\n   102|Barrier-lift route to the topological Bekenstein bound: once the RN barrier is\\n   103|identified with an absolute cocycle-potential increment, nonnegativity follows\\n   104|without carrying an explicit `IsConnesCocycle` packet.\\n   105|-/\\n   106|theorem topologicalBekensteinBound_of_barrierLift\\n   107|  (σ : AdditiveModularFlow (H := H)`
